the inlet is one of the most beautiful landscapes in central europe, located on the danube in hungary, in the neighbourhood of slovakia and austria. the natural values, the richness of flora and fauna, the aquatic habitats and the traditional way of life of the people who live here make it one of the last remaining islands of biodiversity. unfortunately, the water supply of the river that feeds the area has been drastically reduced by hydrological interventions in recent decades. it is in the interest not only of the region concerned, but also of hungary and the whole union, that this unique natural value is not lost for future generations. we have good legislation, such as the water framework directive, which would in principle ensure the protection of the area. however, this would require compliance at both national and eu level. it is desirable that slovakia cooperate with hungary in the normalisation of water supply in the area, independently of the great danube issue. i'd love to take you on a spring-water tour of this fantastic landscape, but it would take water. we're looking for the cooperation of all concerned.
in writing. as the people's party shadow rapporteur on the report in the environment committee, i would like to emphasise that the report is not about the car of the 21st century, but about regulating the automotive industry to be competitive in the 21st century. today, the automotive sector is the second largest emitter of co2 and accounts for 12% of total co2 emissions in the union. the dynamics of this process are alarming, as the sector grew by 26% between 1990 and 2004, accounting for 26.5% of total energy consumption in the union. voluntary agreements by car manufacturers to reduce emissions have not worked and eu regulation is powerless to do anything about it, as the automotive sector can only be covered by the ets after 2013, if at all. the only success was that the average consumption of cars sold in 2004 was 12.4% lower than in 1995, almost entirely due to technological innovation, as cars continued to grow in size and power. further efforts are needed from both member states and consumers. on the one hand, in the area of taxation, to help break into the market for fuel-efficient and low-carbon cars, and to support manufacturers in their targeted development. on the other hand, to improve the provision of information to customers, to modernise and extend labelling to light-duty vehicles, to harmonise energy efficiency classes and to indicate annual running costs. finally, by promoting the practice of bull riding and making it a condition for training, awareness campaigns and driving licences.
on behalf of the ppe-de group. thank you, mr president. i would like to express my deep appreciation to mr and mrs kauppi for the report, which touches on a sensitive and controversial subject of great importance. two comments: firstly, i myself consider it important for the functioning of the internal market that there is no risk that a parent company and a subsidiary are placed at a disadvantage compared to those operating in a single member state simply because they are active in two different member states. i therefore personally call for the elimination of double taxation - as the commissioner has said - including through electronic cooperation mechanisms. in view of the time factor, encourage effective cross-border activity by economic operators, using the method of crediting or discharge. at the same time, i think we have to think about when the subsidiary is profitable and the parent company is losing money. piia-noora kauppi: for example, in the new member states, this is a more interesting situation from our point of view. my other comment, besides the abolition of double taxation, concerns the common consolidated tax base. we're still in the early stages of the debate, but i have reservations: i don't want to be a champion of debt sovereignty, but i don't see the impact of this joint consolidated fund clearly yet. he fears that there will be political pressure to introduce a minimum level, like vat or income tax. this is also shown by the proposal of the socialist and communist, i.e. left-wing, members of parliament, although i believe that the maastricht criteria put a stop to this. but i also have fears about this because i do not know what the impact will be on the new, capital-poor eastern member states in terms of capital flows in the internal market. who's the administration gonna settle with? the rapporteur for the opinion of the regional development committee. - thank you very much, mr president, honourable members. first of all, i'm sorry i'm late, and thank you for letting me speak. the creation of a transparent system for airport charges is of paramount importance, as there is currently no community legislation on this issue. the commission has developed a framework law which sets out how airport charges are to be levied, but which also leaves a certain degree of flexibility to the member states. airports and aviation are of great importance to the european union, as they make a significant contribution to the social and economic cohesion of the european union by connecting regions, creating jobs and attracting businesses. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on 28 june 2010 and entered into force on 1 january 2010. airlines complain that the big airports are acting like a monopoly. the commission considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market if it is granted through state resources and does not adversely affect trading conditions to an extent contrary to the common interest. the fees, which are set differently in each case, should reflect the actual costs of the service or infrastructure used. the differentiation or increase of charges should be based on objective and clear criteria so that airlines and airport users pay justified costs for the same service, at the same price. the consultation mechanism we are introducing is very important because they go to an independent regulator if the airlines are not satisfied with the charges they are charging them. finally, i consider it very important to emphasise that the commission of the european union should on behalf of the pse group. – madam president, first of all i would like to welcome the report and of course i welcome the implementation of the rotterdam convention, which is an important international convention on the ban of several dangerous chemicals. the topic is not unknown to me. i worked as the shadow rapporteur of the pse group on the restriction of several chemicals, including the ban of pfos and the export ban on mercury, and now i and my colleagues are working on the restriction of ammonium nitrate and other substances. we had good cooperation on these issues with my colleague mr blokland. we have to understand that a european-level ban is sometimes not enough. for instance, ddt and lindane have been banned in the eu for many years but their presence can still be detected in the blood of our citizens. following the european ban on certain chemicals, it frequently happens that several multinational companies produce and sell the banned chemicals in non-eu countries, especially in the third world. then the banned chemicals easily return in imported products and they are also present as environmental pollutants in oceans, seas, rivers and air. the export ban on mercury, fully supported by this house, showed us the right reaction. the same treatment is needed for other dangerous chemicals as well. i underline the importance of the international chemical conventions – the rotterdam convention, the stockholm convention and the saicm – as only these instruments can lead to an international solution to the problems caused by chemicals.
the rapporteur. - madam president, commissioner, i have to ask you to leave. before i turn to the minor debates surrounding the amendments, let me say a few words about this legislation and its importance. it is true, as the commissioner has said, that this is a very old piece of legislation, born in 1971, and since then it has traditionally played a prominent role as a supplementary regulatory instrument to the fundamental right of free movement within the union. it is true that the right to free movement of workers enshrined in the treaty would be of little value in itself if citizens in other member states were not able to access social security schemes and if we were unable to ensure the portability of rights. workers exposed to significant risks in the context of intra-union mobility should not be at a disadvantage in terms of social security and fundamental social rights. only then can the free movement of labour fulfil the important balancing role between the eu labour markets that the union economy needs. but we must also see, we must also note, that the regulation 1408, which we are discussing, can only fulfil its function if it is constantly brought into line with national legislation. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the creation of a european network of national experts in the field of employment. that is why it was necessary and it is necessary to constantly amend and supplement the legislation from year to year. it's a key rule, because it seems like we're just reconciling different texts, but it's really about people, about human destinies, about people's everyday problems. we as legislators have a duty to do this, even though we know that this is a text that has been in force for a very short time, because as the commissioner has mentioned, we already have the new regulation, the new directive. until we have an implementing regulation for this, the interests of legal certainty require that we keep updating and adapting the text to changes in national legislation. a good example of this is the first amendment, where the concept of 'close relatives' in hungarian law, the civil code, was amended, and now it was possible to adapt the european text to this. but similarly, the amendments concerning the netherlands, where we are also dealing with very tangible human destinies, soldiers serving abroad, uncertainty about the various social security entitlements for their families. this is clearly unacceptable and needs to be completed. we have, however, found a solution to this problem in the course of the work of the committee, by adopting the oral proposal of the council and incorporating it into the text. so there is no problem here, as i think that the aforementioned amendment no 10 has finally been resolved in a reassuring way, because the dutch government has undertaken to inform the citizens concerned by means of a clarifying circular, so that the adoption of this amendment by parliament is not necessary at this stage. but the cooperation was needed, and i would like to thank all those who took part, both the members who tabled the amendments and the staff of the council and the commission. thank you very much for your time, mr. president.
with the collapse of european borders and the change of lifestyle, there are millions of european citizens who were born in one country, worked in one or more other countries and want to spend their retirement years in another. you pay your social security contributions elsewhere than where you will be claiming them later. the level playing field also requires the harmonisation of social security services. in the longer term, therefore, the creation of a single european social security system, including a pension system, sickness insurance and social security, is inevitable. the hungarian socialist party's working group proposes that this idea be incorporated into the party of european socialists' far-sighted programme. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social security system is harmonized.

mr. chairman, mr. commissioner, my fellow members of the house. europe's future depends on its ability to create societies that embrace and support children. the promotion and protection of children's rights is of crucial importance for the future of the european union. the development of child-friendly societies in the union is inseparable from the further deepening and consolidation of european integration. a comprehensive eu strategy is needed to effectively promote and ensure the rights of children within and outside the union. children shall be entitled to special care and appropriate legal protection. it is the responsibility of the member states to provide parents with a wide range of support in their child-rearing tasks. these are the only tools we have to build a safe and inclusive europe. our policies must constantly take into account the diversity of children, their different needs, with a particular focus on poverty, social exclusion and the effects of discrimination both in the eu and globally. i consider it important that the recommendations of the un committee on the rights of the child are consistently and regularly taken into account in the eu's bilateral agreements with non-eu states. the pse group would like a separate vote on amendment 127.
in writing. – educational segregation is one of the most harmful forms of discrimination faced by roma children. as a basic human right, education is crucial for the attainment of other human rights, and investment in early childhood education for roma is a policy that functions on multiple levels and benefits more than the children it aims to educate. the benefits of early education include: the promotion of social equity, increased individual and broader social productivity, reduced levels of poverty, and the elimination of discriminatory attitudes and social exclusion. as roma children become more educated, they increase their chances of becoming productive members of the workforce. while they earn money and contribute to the national budget through taxes on both income and consumption, they begin to influence the way in which non-roma view them, thereby affecting broader social issues. also, as roma become more productive and their poverty level decreases, they also become contributing members of society instead of beneficiaries of public aid. the combination of the increased contribution and the decreased benefits paid out by the government is the net budgetary benefit to the national budget. a program that supported these developments would benefit all europeans and not only the roma.
in writing. the european union has a major responsibility to protect the fundamental rights of people, and in particular the rights of children. in contrast, 19% of children in the union are at risk of poverty, a higher proportion than among the adult population (15%). worldwide, 40 million children under the age of 12 are victims of some form of violence. nearly six million are in forced labor, and one and a half million are victims of human trafficking. in view of these developments, a european strategy for the protection of children's rights with zero tolerance is urgently needed. i believe that better regulation, including the impact of new and existing rules on minors, can play a key role in this. however, i do not support the creation of a separate parliamentary body responsible for children's rights. the commission shall be assisted by a coordinator for children's rights, appointed by the commission, who may also carry out its tasks. i am in favour of the creation of a post of commissioner for human rights and minorities, who would also be responsible for the protection of children's rights. it is important to note that the majority of disadvantaged children are roma or belong to minorities in europe. it would also be advisable to implement a european institutional reform in which the child rights coordinators under the commissioner would become a link between the dialogue and cooperation between institutions, ngos and governments. given that there are already a number of european organisations and institutions dealing with children's rights in the union, the emphasis should be on bringing together and making the old ones more effective, rather than creating new ones. today, education is becoming a more pressing issue along with the protection of children's rights. mindful parenting turns unwitting child offenders into conscious law-abiding citizens.

author. - thank you very much. mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i would remind you that the lack of integration of the roma was already brought to the attention of this parliament in 1983. in 1994, the european parliament adopted a resolution calling on the commission, the council and the governments of the member states to do their utmost to promote the social, economic and political integration of the roma. as you will recall, in the parliament's resolution adopted in april 2005, we asked the commission to adopt an action plan containing clear recommendations for the member states and candidate countries on the economic, social and political integration of roma. then, in november 2007, on my proposal, the issue of roma social integration was again included in the text of the parliament's resolution on free movement as a european-wide roma strategy. we then asked the commission again to develop a european strategy for the social integration of roma without delay, using the integration fund and the structural funds. in the meantime, we know for sure that in at least ten eu member states, roma children are still forced to study in segregated classrooms and segregated institutions, and as a matter of common practice, they are unjustifiably classified as disabled, and placed on a certain life path, forever stigmatising us, roma. unfortunately, in every member state, it is known exactly in which slums the roma live, in which terrible conditions. i regret to say that we also know for certain that the average life expectancy of roma in all member states is 15 years lower than that of eu citizens. again, in the member states, roma are overrepresented among the unemployed in all member states. in the meantime, unfortunately, not a month goes by without a racist attack on roma in a member state. we know from reports - or we may not have known specifically - that on the night of january 4, 2008, a shelter in the marconi district of rome was set on fire to nearly 250 roma living there, and then three days later, in aprilia, italy, molotov cocktails were thrown at a local cigar factory, putting nearly hundreds of people in immediate danger. in both cases, the attacks were motivated by racism. there is no doubt that accountability is a right, that a motion for a resolution is needed, that a european strategy is needed and that each member state should draw up an action plan for roma integration. no one is an exception, no one can pretend that there are no roma in their own country - whether they are recognised or not - and the above would not be true for all eu member states, without exception. thank you, sir.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i was pleased to hear commissioner ferrero-waldner speak and i was pleased to see the initiatives that are expected to take place in the future. i think that this will really help the situation of the roma, but i have to agree with my dutch colleague who has expressed his impatience. i myself and many others are impatient because the situation is changing very slowly. the 7 to 9 million roma living in europe, most of them in poor social conditions, face the same problems as they did years ago: the problem of exclusion and integration, the lack of employment, the problem of segregation in schools, the multiple discrimination against roma women. most member states do not consider roma as a national minority, do not want to achieve any substantial change in their situation, and the experience of recent years has shown a rather stronger radicalism and anti-gypsyism in both the new and the old member states. i therefore believe that we need to stop wasting time and make real changes, and that we need a coordinated european roma policy. without a european roma policy, there is no national roma policy, and we must understand and face this. it would be good if we took steps to recognise the transnational minority status of the roma, because it is indeed a special status. the european parliament and the socialist group have already launched an action plan, and in march we will be travelling around europe, organising conferences, workshops and visits involving local roma, ngos and governments, in order to uncover cases of discrimination and to draw the attention of the european public to the plight of the roma and to try to remedy it. thank you very much.

in writing. – renewable energies can, in principle, offer a solution to global climate change and europe’s energy crisis. however, biofuels and other forms of bioenergy are contradictory tools for combating climate change. up to a certain level they positively replace fossil energy, but on a larger scale they endanger the rainforests and the food supply of third world countries. there are already worrying signs of deforestation in brazil, indonesia, malaysia and other countries. food prices have risen as well due to the growing consumption of biofuels. the eu should limit its bioenergy consumption to the surplus agricultural capacity of the union itself, discouraging deforestation.
madam president! by the end of 2012, the full liberalisation of postal services will be completed, bringing the common market in this area to completion. since the opening of the market will have a different impact on the markets of the member states, we have managed in the course of the legislation to make the opening step by step. we have also achieved what i personally consider to be a result, namely that the posts concerned will be able to retain their current available revenue until the end of the derogation period. for the first time in a long time, european regulation was designed to put everyone in a competitive position. special thanks to the reporter for that. in other words, for companies competing after the opening of the market, this regulation does not represent a disadvantage but also new prospects. madam president, please. but all of this is only half a success if the next steps are not taken. what are they? first, the post with a derogation must focus in the coming years on being able to meet the requirements of european competition, i.e. to make real use of the time advantage that must now be gained. second, national regulations and policies must ensure that, while the postal service is being slimmed down with the slogan of competitiveness, universal service is actually provided at an adequate level. because let's not forget, the responsibility for providing it remains the responsibility of the nation state. the regulation was designed to ensure that european citizens, wherever they live, even in the smallest towns, have access to postal services at an appropriate price and quality. thank you for your attention.
thank you, madam president, and colleagues. the gradual opening of the market for postal services is an important milestone towards the completion of the internal market. it contributes to the abolition of special rights within the postal sectors, sets a specific and irrevocable date for opening, and ensures a sustainable high standard of universal services. opening up markets increases competition and thus improves the level of service, quality, price and choice. the measure will contribute to the harmonisation of the basic principles governing postal services and is expected to result in lower tariffs, better and more innovative services and enhanced conditions for growth and employment in the sector. the amendment of the directive is the result of an exemplary compromise, one which took account of the differences arising from the historical and economic characteristics of the member states. this is in view of the fact that preparation for liberalisation takes longer in some member states, especially in central and eastern europe. at the same time, taking into account the interests of others, in order to avoid distortions of competition in the market, postal operators in countries where the postal sector is already fully liberalised may not provide services until the end of the derogation period in december 2012. i thank my colleague, mr. ferber, for his work, but i also thank, of course, shadow reporter brian simpson.

in writing. – the aim of creating a dynamic and innovative society in europe bringing growth and employment by 2010 will be successfully achieved only through an approach that genuinely takes into account the fight against poverty and the social dimension. while refocusing the growth and jobs strategy on an improved performance-based approach, social inclusion and environmental standards should at no cost be left aside for achieving economic targets. the prospect of creating new jobs in an innovative-based economy should bring developments in the fields of education and training to improve the equal integration of the most vulnerable and disadvantaged categories of the population eu-wide. investments on innovation, research, transport and energy efficiency should focus on bringing real benefits to the european consumers in a free and fair trade economic union. as a matter of fact, economic growth is not an end in itself and, of course, economic figures are not to be confused with the welfare of european citizens. the real aim of the lisbon strategy should in the end still be the improvement of the quality of life for our citizens and future generations; therefore, a strong social dimension is required.
in writing. the ten-year period in which the european union was supposed to become the most competitive economic area in the world, with a strong social network and exemplary environmental performance, is coming to an end. it doesn't take much courage to predict that in the next two years, we won't be able to perform miracles. the undertaking was too ambitious, and the results were rather modest. but when you consider that in recent years the european union has achieved historic enlargement, reuniting europe, creating a common currency and uniform legislation for 27 nations in many areas, there is no reason to be disappointed with the achievement. in many respects, europe continues to set an example for the world in terms of green thinking and legislation. but we know very well that there are contradictions in this area as well. many laws only prescribe malaszt in some member states, and some environmental limits are more permissible than in many non-eu countries. the lisbon objectives are therefore correct, but much more needs to be done to achieve them - after the great enlargement boom, in a period of consolidation - than has been the case so far.

mr. president, i speak on behalf of a region in demographic crisis and one of its sickest nations. as a member of the committee responsible for public health, i cannot stress enough how closely our demographic problems are intertwined with our health problems. in my country, hungary, cancer is the leading cause of death in europe, and it contributes to the fact that every year 50% more people die than are born. the castex report on the demographic future of europe rightly points to the need to develop new policies for demographic renewal. let me give you just one example: while the population of islamic countries will grow to 1,300 million by 2050, europe's will shrink from 495 to 400 million. this new demographic policy must be built on two foundations: the family as the most important social unit and healthy people. families need to be provided with effective incentives and protection. today, in many member states, having a child is tantamount to accepting poverty. but there are many good examples. in some countries, the state takes the extra burden off the shoulders of large families. this good example must be implemented everywhere. health is the condition for quality of life. citizens should be made interested in preserving their own health. to do this, they receive the necessary state support both in terms of money (see compulsory screenings, free sports facilities) and in terms of mental health and demographic education. it is in the vital interest of each member state to improve its demographic and health situation. this is particularly important in hungary, where the population may fall below 10 million by next year, and where the average age is ten years behind the eu average. the future of europe depends on the demographic performance of the generations living today. what a government misses today with bad policy, it will be hard to fix later.
in writing. population ageing poses significant economic, budgetary and social challenges. it will have a serious impact on growth and will put significant pressure on social spending, so preparing for it is crucial. addressing the demographic challenge is therefore a multifaceted challenge that requires complex strategic analysis, planning and social dialogue. it is not just a question of gradual population reduction, but also of the sustainability of the european social model, which is aimed at the well-being of european citizens, the economic development of europe, the guarantee of its global competitiveness at international level, in other words, the future of europe, our future, and the search for solutions is a common interest. the member states of the european union should support demographic change by all possible means, based on concrete measures to ensure the sustainability of more complex solidarity relationships between generations, the practical implementation of equal opportunities, reconciliation of work and private life, full implementation of the barcelona objectives, the creation of active ageing conditions and the social inclusion of immigrants and ethnic minorities. only common goals, common interests, common solidarity and a commitment to achieving the goals can bring the desired results.

for example, registration tax - is a good idea. the second. the expansion of the trade in pollution is another cost factor that will be reflected in prices. this should also be taken into account in the idea of introducing indirect taxes, which is sometimes proposed. the third. indeed, efforts should be made to reduce congestion in cities and thus emissions of air pollution. however, the biggest challenge for the new member states is not only to support urban electric public transport but perhaps even more so to build bypass roads. they are relatively the most lagging behind and should be highlighted as one of the priorities for the use of eu funds. 4. to i agree with the need to create sector neutrality in the internalisation of external costs. however, the requirement of the eu matrix is already a major challenge for hauliers in the new member states, due to increasingly stringent engine emission standards, etc. so internalization in our country can only be solved more slowly and according to specific principles. our hauliers cannot be penalized for example because they have less developed infrastructure and therefore more traffic jams. the fifth. it would be good if the new member states, particularly the danube and the tisza, were to help in the shipping sector, not just with words but also with deeds, and the commission would help in this. intermodality is important and can be the biggest help in modernising the rail network. let's talk about that, and not count the railroad tracks. finally, i hope that the construction of high-speed railways will eventually put the eastern region on the map, because it can only compete with the passenger car in the long term. thank you very much.
madam president, i have to ask you to leave. humans have never traveled as much as we do today. forced relocation is also a huge waste of time, a major economic problem and a serious environmental burden. one third of the energy we use is used for transport. and in europe, our main mode of transport is the passenger car. in the last 10 years, the number of cars per thousand inhabitants has increased from 400 to 460, and the use of passenger cars has increased by 18%. over the past 15 years, co2 emissions from transport have increased dynamically, by more than 100% in some member states. the commission forecasts that the energy demand for transport in the union will increase by at least 30% by 2030. this is the kyoto protocol's downfall. it is regrettable that, contrary to the principles that have been set out, road transport projects continue to be given priority in europe over rail projects. the bad examples are compelling, and the former hub of public transport in central and eastern europe has also changed. in my country, hungary, in 2007, public transport only accounted for 40 percent of the total. reducing pollution caused by motor vehicles is a matter of traffic management, technology and economics. the spread of green technologies depends on the internalisation of environmental costs and the introduction of a carbon taxation system. at airports, differentiated take-off and landing charges are required, depending on the emissions of the aircraft. free parking for green cars, reduced congestion and road fees, and lower vehicle taxes, levies and entry fees could trigger a spectacular transformation. health care costs from transportation are also staggering. it's not fair to make society pay for all of this. the costs of air pollution, noise pollution and the resulting accidents should be borne by transport, i believe. thank you, sir.

thank you, sir. the increase in milk quotas is premature and risky in the current market situation for two reasons. firstly, the market outlook is not nearly as favourable as the european commission assumes. recent developments have demonstrated the risks of quota increases. the increase in the price of milk products has been welcomed by consumers in europe and beyond. consumption decreased by 10-30% in several countries and imports of chinese milk powder decreased. as a result, the purchase price of milk in hungary, for example, has fallen by 10-20% in recent weeks. the main reason may be that meat prices have not kept pace with other food prices, so consumers are eating meat instead of rising dairy prices to meet their protein needs. on the other hand, there are significant unused milk quotas in many member states. the increase in the quota would prevent producers in those member states from exploiting their opportunities and could even lead to a reduction in production in those countries, as producers in the member states using the quota would buy the livestock needed to expand production from those countries. so for now, there's no need to increase the quota, let's wait and analyze the situation. if price increases and demand growth continue over the longer term, let's return to the question.

in writing. – of course it is of the utmost importance to clearly stress in the framework of this report the necessity to maintain direct payments not only in the case of climate-related crisis or market failures but also to balance fairly the high european standards in terms of environmental, animal and consumer protection. nevertheless, it is our duty to consider seriously the situation of the least-developed agricultural producing countries while reviewing the criteria under which direct payments will be made in the future under our common agricultural policy. moreover, we have to acknowledge the difficult situation faced by most of the new member states’ agricultural sectors requiring specific attention and further investments mainly in restructuring and modernisation of the sector. finally, i would like to point out that it is essential while addressing the cap health check that the emphasis should – following the recent crises – be on guaranteeing food safety and supply for our citizens by ensuring sufficient food-stocks.
in writing. there is a widening gap in employment between urban and rural areas. disadvantages are particularly acute for rural women. they have less favourable chances of social integration, employment, access to childcare, education, health and social services. they are one of the most vulnerable groups in society, increasingly at risk of poverty and marginalisation. even if they can get settled, their earnings are significantly lower than those of their urban counterparts. the cumulative emergence of social disadvantages creates social tensions, since it is precisely in the most disadvantaged municipalities that the social care system is most deficient and the infrastructure deficiencies are greatest. the role of agriculture, with its short-term benefits and lower standard of living, preserves the tense employment situation of the countryside. the solution is to work together, and so local authorities, governments and the european community have a responsible role to play. complex containment programs are required. opportunities for rural people to catch up should be created through the creation of alternative jobs, infrastructure developments, the launch of market-oriented training and retraining programmes, support for business start-ups, and the establishment of border and cross-border projects. it is inevitable that catch-up programmes will be tailored to the specificities of each rural area. this can ensure that territorial differences are continuously reduced.

the rapporteur. - mr president, this is officer fischer-boel. my fellow officers. in a world of climate change, rising energy demands and energy prices, reducing energy dependence and consumption, and diversifying energy sources, is an absolute priority, a key interest of the union. the increasing use of renewable energy sources is an integral part of this. we need to diversify, and it's worth it, within renewables. in the field of renewable energy sources, different countries have different capacities, which we should exploit to the fullest. biogas has a huge potential and a great future as a renewable energy source, for a number of reasons listed in the report we are discussing today. first and foremost, ladies and gentlemen, the main raw material for biogas is animal manure, and its processing for environmental purposes and energy production can be linked. this is one of the most important benefits of biogas production, although of course biogas can be produced not only from animal manure but also from municipal sludge, slaughterhouse waste and power plants. biogas is a multifunctional energy source: it can be used for electricity, heating, cooling and drying. the compressed biogas is suitable for the propulsion of cars, public transport vehicles and can be fed into the natural gas network after proper purification. biogas production is therefore a major opportunity for european energy production in this sense. among other things, if we look at the possibility of producing biogas from animal manure, we could produce 14 times more than we are producing at the moment. this is a very serious potential, because from an environmental point of view, we know that we cannot put animal manure on the land without proper processing, because that would be very serious environmental damage. there was a debate in our committee about whether biogas production, when it uses plant-based products, is not a competitor to food production. the european commission has shown that this is not the case in europe today, and we hope it will not be, with the second generation of power plants. this may be a problem globally, in brazil, in the united states, but it's not a problem in europe today, and hopefully it won't be a problem. the other debate in this regard is how do we regulate it? the current regulation, both at national and eu level, is extremely complex, over-regulated, which is why i proposed a biogas directive in the report. however, i have been assured by the european commission officials, and in consultation with the european people's party, that there is no need for a separate biogas directive, but that a directive on renewable energy sources is being prepared, and that the biogas problem can be solved within this framework. i would ask the commission to pay particular attention to the renewable energy directive, to use the suggestions in my report. my fellow members of the house of representatives! in my reply, i would like to thank the danish institute for its help and all those who have contributed to this. thank you for your attention.

on behalf of the pse group. – madam president, i debated about whether to speak in hungarian or english on this subject and then i realised that during the working process i used mainly english expressions on the given topic. the pse group had an intensive debate on the report and reached a compromise standpoint. some of us, including myself, had more radical amendments but the common position of the group is acceptable to us. i should like to welcome the report and congratulate the rapporteur. pesticides, on the one hand, are an important and essential support to modern european agriculture but, on the other hand, the non-sufficient pesticide use and the use of harmful substances are responsible for health problems, fatal accidents and environmental pollution. decision-makers, authorities, local governments, farmers, ngos and citizens need proper information regarding pesticide use in order to reduce and prevent the negative impacts of pesticide use. therefore, this proposal for european statistics on pesticides is a positive step forward towards environmentally friendly agriculture. based on european statistics, we can monitor and evaluate the positive changes provided for in new pesticide legislation, i.e. the directive on pesticide use, the thematic strategy on sustainable pesticide use and the regulation on pesticide authorisations. from the beginning, i proposed and supported the inclusion of biocide products into this directive with the approval of the pse group. as in most cases, the active ingredient of the biocides is also used as a plant production product. biocides therefore have almost the same health and environmental impact. it does not make any difference after it has been released into the environment whether the substance was produced as biocide or as a plant production product. it is reasonable that, first, we collect the statistics on the biocide products only. business confidentiality is always a sensitive issue and, therefore, i am pleased we all accept that this directive takes into account the obligations under the aarhus convention on access to environmental information. that is why i supported the amendment that obliges the producers to provide proper information for the public. i hope that after the compromise among the political parties represented in this chamber, we will have a good first reading agreement from the council and within a few years, perhaps, i will be able to quote here in this chamber the useful data from the latest european pesticides statistics.
thank you, madam president. there is no doubt that pesticides against various pests and insects have fundamentally reformed european agriculture. however, these chemicals have adverse effects on human health and the environment. therefore, plant protection products that are particularly dangerous for humans should be withdrawn from the market and the use of chemicals for this purpose should be significantly reduced. however, this requires reliable statistics that are comparable and harmonised at eu level. we do not want to put a burden on consumers, on farms, that is not our goal. but it is important to include all non-agricultural uses of pesticides in the statistics and thus in the scope of future regulation. let me give you an example. in europe, stone scrap along railway tracks is sprayed with a pesticide, an isopropilamine salt, or rather washed. the eu's rail network is about 213,000 km long, and it is estimated that 900,000 litres of chemicals are sprayed on it every year. this, along with rainwater, enters our groundwater and surface water, eventually entering our bodies. we haven't paid enough attention to this, and that needs to change. the report before us is an integral part of the legislative package discussed last year and should be treated as such. the framework directive on the sustainable use of plant protection products, which was noted by my colleague, christa klaß, is the thematic strategy on the sustainable use of pesticides, written this year by belohorská, and the regulation on the placing of plant protection products on the market, whereby hiltrud breyer, the european parliament's thematic coordinator, has set the direction for the future of the sustainable use of plant protection products. i congratulate the rapporteur and support the report. thank you very much.

in writing. union support for district heating! i welcome the european commission's initiative for a new energy efficiency and renewable energy source. one of the greatest challenges of the 21st century is to ensure that we have an adequate supply of energy without compromising our environmental values, our strategic security or our competitiveness. the cheapest energy, of course, is the energy we don't use, so it's the energy we save. energy efficiency, in this age of energy quality, is a fundamental concern for everyone, both consumers and the wider community. the left-wing view is that energy conservation, energy efficiency, is also a social issue, because often the poorest people waste the most energy. this is particularly true for residential homes in the new member states, such as hungary, where they use more than twice as much energy per square metre as in western europe. it is therefore important to provide community support for the modernisation of outdated district heating systems in eastern europe. district heating is in principle an energy-saving system, with the particular advantage of being easily convertible to renewable energy, which in the case of individual heating has to be solved per household. therefore, it is crucial to ensure that district heating receives union support. in addition, a single eu tax credit could be envisaged to help create energy saving investments and funds.

in writing. i would like to point out that while women make up approximately 4 to 5% of prison inmates in europe and the proportion of female prisoners is increasing in many european countries, sometimes even exceeding the number of male prisoners, prisons are still set up for men and tend to ignore the specific problems of an increasing proportion of women prisoners. the areas of greatest concern are health, the situation of mothers with children, and professional and social reintegration. statistics on female prisoners generally show low levels of education and lack of skills. if we want to ensure that the social integration of female prisoners is more stable after their years in prison, we should offer all prisoners the opportunity to acquire basic educational skills. however, in many european prisons, there are differences between the vocational training provided to women and the demands of the labour market. most prisons offer feminized vocational training limited to the development of knowledge and skills related to the cultural and social roles traditionally attributed to women. these skills, which are generally poorly paid, are not highly valued in the labour market, and this contributes to the persistence of social inequalities and may undermine social and professional reintegration. prison authorities should therefore be encouraged to provide, even in cooperation with external companies, high quality vocational training adapted to labour market needs, a variety of employment opportunities free from gender-based bias.

in writing. the european commission's green paper on mitigating the negative effects of climate change is a welcome development. however forward-looking the eu's proposals to combat climate change may be, we must live with the consequences of changing climate today. the available data indicate that some countries are more affected by the rate of accelerating warming. these include hungary, where the rate of change has been well above average in recent decades. the population was able to see firsthand that this was not just an abstract phenomenon: the number of droughts increased, the amount of rainfall decreased, and this had a significant impact on agricultural production. perhaps the most spectacular manifestations of climate extremes in the carpathian basin have been the record floods of the past decade. we are convinced that flood protection is one of the areas where urgent and consistent action is needed. the european union and the member states must pay greater attention to ensuring that the ever-smaller and ever-larger rainfall does not simultaneously burden rivers. to this end, natural water retention potential should be mapped in flat areas and the risk of flooding reduced by afforestation in mountainous areas. let's hope that decision-makers don't wait for the shocking news of the next flood to take action.

on behalf of the alde group. mr president, officer, please. ukraine and belarus play a key role in the eastern dimension of our neighbourhood policy. in the case of ukraine, the goal is democracy, market economy and consolidation of european orientation, and in the case of belarus, the goal is the democratisation of the country. the granting of travel facilities is an effective and efficient means of doing so. ukrainian citizens have been able to obtain visas under a simplified procedure since january 1, but most of them feel that their situation is more difficult than before, as they can reach their important destinations, poland, hungary, lithuania, with greater difficulties than before. this is perceived as a step backwards, as the eur 35 visa fee is a large sum and the administrative burden and the administrative procedure are extremely complex. in order to ensure the effective implementation of the visa waiver, the commission should be able to request the competent authorities of ukraine to provide the necessary information to the competent authorities of the member states. as far as belarus is concerned, we must make it clear that europe's problem is not with the belarusian people but with the oppressive system, so we want to make it easier for belarusian citizens to travel. this requires a reduction in the visa fee of eur 60, which is extremely high, since it corresponds to almost half of the average monthly income in belarus, and at the same time a reduction in the administrative burden, since these complex requirements are difficult to meet. this will help to ensure that the citizens of both ukraine and belarus feel closer to europe. thank you, sir.

in writing. i welcome the independent report by mr adamou on organ donation and trafficking. although unfortunately the expertise of the member states on this important issue varies widely, the use of human organs for transplantation has increased rapidly throughout the european union in recent decades. in western europe, there are now nearly 40,000 people on waiting lists, and as a result, the livelihood trade in organs is booming in poorer areas of eastern european countries. the european donor card can be a good solution to legal issues related to donation, but more serious action is needed to combat illegal and commercially motivated organ trafficking. given that, despite isolated attempts, there is no comprehensive register of available transplantable organs in many places, even at national level, it would be important to introduce a uniform eu transplant database, similar to the schengen system, accessible on the internet, and a medical certificate valid throughout the union. legally certified human organ transplantation facilities could access life-saving information in a matter of seconds, facilitate bilateral agreements, and protect the poorest and most vulnerable donors from becoming victims of the organ trade. the establishment of the database is essential to enable the bodies to be used in more than one country, and it is therefore essential that member states abolish their legislation prohibiting this as soon as possible. it is also important that organ donation remains strictly 'non-commercial' but that the conditions under which financial compensation may be granted are also defined.
, in writing. in europe, more than 100 million civilian volunteers work for the benefit of others and the public interest. in the uk, 38% of the total population and in poland 18% of the population are engaged in voluntary activities. voluntary activity is inseparable from active european citizenship, which can be seen as a basic principle of democracy at both local and community level. citizens also contribute to solving social problems through volunteering, their activities help to develop social awareness and skills. through the work of volunteers, different groups of the population can have the opportunity to catch up and integrate, while the elderly, for example, can participate in society, benefit from their life experience and feel useful for a longer time. voluntary activities are motivated by the fundamental european values of solidarity and responsibility. the usefulness of volunteering is also increasingly recognised within the eu and voluntary organisations are being more closely involved in decision-making processes. in recent years, the scope of voluntary work has become even more diversified. the interest in volunteering is growing, the demand is growing, but there is a lack of appreciation, limited financial resources, and a lack of infrastructure. that needs to change. in order to support voluntary work and increase its social acceptance, it is important to have a community of interest based on cooperation, dialogue, partnership, sharing of best practices and experience between the actors concerned, such as the state, businesses, trade unions and voluntary organisations.
thank you very much, mr. president. i'd like to deal with the report from the european agencies. the european parliament examines the management of the european agencies on an annual basis and relies on the work of both the european court of auditors and the european anti-fraud office to carry out this task. as a result of a long and thorough analysis and preparatory work carried out together, we have reached a decision on the management of the community agencies for 2006 and we are very pleased to recommend all agencies, in agreement with the european court of auditors, for adoption. this does not mean that we have not found errors in the management, but it does mean that we have not found errors of a degree that would impede acceptance. i would also like to point out that the groups in the committee voted by a very large democratic majority to recommend that the work of the agencies be accepted here in plenary, even if the rapporteur does not entirely agree with his own report. thank you for listening.
thank you very much, mr. president. ladies and gentlemen, in 2006, the european union spent 39% of its common budget, some €38.9 billion, on structural expenditure. the european court of auditors found in its annual report that the european commission had made a number of progresses in the use of the structural and cohesion funds and in overall control. financial absorption has clearly improved, but the report points out that at least 12% of payments for structural projects in 2006 were not reimbursable. this is almost eur 4 billion, and 20% of the projects were defective. over the years, a number of problems have arisen with the multi-level and shared management of the structural funds. the relatively high rate of detected irregularities and incorrect refunds is mainly due to the low efficiency of the member states' control systems and the control systems based on them. the biggest problem i see is that there is no uniform, universally accepted evaluation model, no necessary indicator or benchmarking system, and no effective measurement of projects. although the structural funds provide adequate resources for very important joint developments, the reduction in the number of misuses of public funds can only be achieved if the member states and the european commission act together in a consensus to reduce the illegal use of money. there is also a need to improve the effectiveness and harmonise the control systems of the member states. it is also essential that the commission makes its controls on structural expenditure more effective, applies the suspension and adjustment procedures and develops a new system of reporting by the member states, given that 40% of commission audits have been problematic. in the light of the above, our delegation proposes to accept the discharge. thank you for listening.
thank you, mr president. as the shadow rapporteur for the envi opinion on discharge 2006, i would like to thank jutta haug and the rapporteur for their thorough work. i believe that the work of our four agencies is very important and that their work undoubtedly helps the european parliament and the other eu institutions to do their job and thus improve the quality of life of our citizens. the european centre for disease prevention and control plays an important role in epidemiological surveillance and in the formulation of opinions on the risk of new and emerging epidemic diseases. perhaps this is also the reason that hungary has already become the first country in terms of immunity to epidemics this year, since we have the least number of people infected with diseases that can be prevented by vaccination. the work of the european environment agency is essential in the collection and synthesis of environmental information, and the european medicines agency plays a major role in the protection of public and animal health. i consider the agency's contribution to the development of new medicines to be particularly important. the european food safety authority provides essential support in the areas of food safety, animal health and plant health. it is important to note that all agencies have shifted their annual budgets, which is due to the complexity of the tasks, as the rapporteur points out. this is why it is worth putting more emphasis on this when planning for the future. i welcome the rapporteur's suggestions on the importance of internal control, on strengthening it, on the need to remedy the shortage of human resources that is arising here. finally, i would like to draw attention to the importance of the commission's work on clarifying the role of the agencies, which is now beginning. agencies are needed, but transparency and efficiency require clarification of their place in the governance of the union. thank you very much.

the rapporteur. - mr president! - what are you doing? honourable president of the council, minister žerjav, president barrot, colleagues. the european parliament has consistently and strongly supported the galileo programme from the outset. this was the case last november, when the european parliament took a bold step to bring the programme out of the deadlock, and then in december, when funding for the gnss programmes was approved from the revised community budget. the regulation implementing the galileo and egnos gnss programmes is now being discussed following a successful informal trial run at first reading. financing entirely from the community budget requires a special responsibility towards the taxpaying european citizens and the correspondingly careful regulation. this is particularly true for funding, programme management, procurement procedures and security. we've learned a lot in the last few years as we've faced the problems. however, in terms of regulation, we have taken a completely new path, a new path, so to speak, because this is the first common european infrastructure that we are building together and that will be publicly owned by the community. financing: the european parliament notes that the cost of implementing the programme is eur 3.4 billion, which is provided by the budgetary authorities. it also calls on the commission to submit a proposal by 2010 on financing beyond 2013, and in particular on public liabilities. it is important to note that, for the economic success of the programmes, adherence to the timetable and the avoidance of further delays are of paramount importance. the pricing policy will be determined after a detailed analysis of the financing of the recovery phase, taking into account the high quality of the service provided to consumers. we have built the governance of the programme on a clear division of powers. the commission shall be responsible for the implementation of the programme as manager of the programme. its work shall be assisted by the gnss programme committee, in a comitological procedure. the european space agency is the so-called 'prime contractor' and it should be noted that a further guarantee of the success of the programme is the appropriate contractual agreement between the commission and esa. and after a long debate, the gsa is essentially the security function. the european parliament, the council and the commission recognised that new ways of working together were needed to make the programme a success. therefore, in accordance with the declaration in the annex to the regulation, we will in future monitor the implementation of the programme together on a quarterly basis, in the framework of the so-called galileo interinstitutional panel, and the annual review of the work programme. the rules on public procurement procedures were already of high importance in the november rapporteur's report and the essential elements of these were incorporated in the council proposal. we agree with the latter. with galileo, we promised new technology, development, new entrepreneurial opportunities and jobs, and in particular we promoted the participation of smes in front of entrepreneurs in the 27 member states. the regulations provide for that. the parliament's negotiating delegation, led by angelika niebler, president of the itre committee, and including representatives of the shadow rapporteurs from the opinion committees, presented the text adopted in the informal trilogue at first reading to the itre committee. the itre committee unanimously adopted it with one abstention. on this basis, we are now submitting the regulation to the ep plenary. finally, i would like to thank the slovenian presidency for its commitment to the programme, the expertise of the commission and the support of my colleagues which made it possible to reach an agreement. thank you, sir.

on behalf of the pse group. "the world can be different!" - that's what the leftists say, who think that our world can be changed. we must not get involved in casino capitalism, so-called development that destroys natural resources. the history of the 20th century, however, teaches us that utopias are doomed to failure without adequate economic and social backing. the green tax, the environmental foundation of all taxation policy, is a market-based, sustainable tool for a better and more livable world. as for the increased taxation of raw materials, the single energy tax could force us to reduce our dependence on energy imports, to drive less, to reinvent public transport, the electricity grid, the railway. if we shift the focus of taxation from labour income to consumption, we create a lot of new jobs and we give more importance to a way of life closer to nature and culture than to consumerism. emissions of pollutants, taxation of non-recyclable waste reduces pollution, the associated public diseases, reduces premature and unnecessary death. the transition to green taxation can also be seen as a peaceful revolution. of course, the level of taxation cannot be increased, because europe is already taxing too much. reducing energy consumption would also increase the competitiveness of our goods. green taxes can move us from the extremely materialistic world of the 20th century to a more environmentally friendly, more human-centric, more intelligent world. if there is one issue that should unite us, the european representatives of 27 countries, it is the issue of green taxation. the world can be different, but only through thoughtful and bold reforms.
in writing. in order to achieve environmental objectives, member states now favour almost exclusively direct requirements. this is despite the fact that a number of other regulatory instruments are possible. among these, we believe that market-based environmental incentives should receive priority attention. we believe that the market, competition and environmental protection are not mutually exclusive. the importance of market incentives lies in the fact that they achieve the environmental quality they set as an objective in a cheaper and more efficient manner. they may also contribute to the achievement of other social objectives. unfortunately, the european union has done little to promote market-based incentives. perhaps the emissions trading system is the only exception. it is therefore welcome that the green paper we are discussing is exploring the potential of these incentives. but it's worth adding that mapping is a long way ahead of us. the wider use of market-based regulators should be accompanied by the elimination of direct regulations that lead to significant inefficiencies. since economic incentives also have a revenue-generating effect, we need to examine how these instruments can trigger taxes, which are primarily labour-intensive. it is of particular importance that this subject be discussed in depth. we are convinced that ambitious eu environmental policy objectives cannot be achieved without the use of market incentives.

, in writing. in the last 50 years, the world's population has doubled, and energy consumption has quadrupled. the commission has therefore no choice but to welcome and support by all means the creation of the fuel cell and hydrogen joint undertaking. i understand the concerns of mrs haug about funding, but the financials should not stand in the way of this initiative. the key words are innovation and lifestyle change. and that costs money. but it's worth it. in modern economies, innovation accounts for 70 to 80 percent of annual gdp growth! why would we invest in hydrogen? because it's an intermediate energy carrier. its "stocks" are endless, the most abundant element in the universe. however, it can only be considered as an alternative to fossil fuels if it can be produced from renewable energy sources at a competitive price and without emitting carbon dioxide or other greenhouse gases into the atmosphere. the importance of this was recognized in canada and the united states as early as 2002. there are hundreds of thousands of "biomills" on the roads, and the number of hydrogen stations is growing. there are around 18 000 environmentally friendly models in the union. the charging station coverage is not even worth mentioning. to avoid the final collapse we cannot wait with this technology and fuel support, we must act. i am also inviting my colleagues to my green innovation conference in parliament on the afternoon of 28 may next week, where, after the meeting, participants will be able to travel by hydrogen bus and hydrogen car.
, in writing. – we welcome the ongoing efforts to further incorporate the level of consumers' awareness as the necessary basis for the effective implementation of the existing consumer protection legal framework, especially with regard to the most vulnerable groups of the population. not only is there a need for a more comprehensive legal framework for consumers' rights, but also we ought genuinely to take into consideration consumers’ interests when completing the internal market standardisation. it is obvious that a stronger, unique consumer protection system effective all over the union is in the interest of all citizens. the completion of the internal market is an opportunity to implement harmonisation measures so as to target the problems faced by consumers in their everyday life. specific attention should therefore be given to sectors related to the new digital environment. a good consumer is a consumer aware of his or her rights and how rightfully to enforce them. we ought to develop in the community a genuine consumer culture as part of our education system and to systematically involve consumer organizations in the consultations and the dialogue with industry. we welcome therefore the call to establish a special european consumer ombudsman in the european ombudsman’s office on a country-by-country basis.
, in writing. the employment directive is a long-term guideline for the member states of the enlarged europe, setting out the objectives and means for the second half of the implementation of the lisbon strategy to increase competitiveness and employment. it has since been proven that no competitive, efficient economy in a competitive world can move faster than others if it is to be built in the social desert among people who are insecure and threatened with social exclusion. decent work is a prerequisite for a decent life, and this requires potential workers who are adequately qualified, able to renew their knowledge, in good health and protected from discrimination. in 2006, the directive was revised to take account of the needs of the new member states. this gave impetus to the cumulative disadvantage in the labour market, the hopeless labour market situation of non-young women, the specific linguistic isolation and the issue of roma employment. in the last two years, the number of jobs has increased, the employment rates have improved, and the dramatic activity indicators have become milder. however, the growth of jobs in the classic full-time contract jobs was slower, while the share of part-time employment, seasonal work and contract work rose exponentially. in the changed situation, we do not argue that the time for dogmatic labour law regulation is over. extensive and intensive economic development requires flexible rules which must not lead to a relativism of labour law and to a de-emphasis of the importance of social partnership and collective agreements.
on behalf of the pse group. thank you, mr president. elizabeth lynne's excellent report is based on the responsibility that comes with the discrimination we live with. not only has this not disappeared from the europe of excellence, but according to eurobarometer surveys, it is increasing and citizens are feeling it. i would add that with enlargement new phenomena of discrimination threats have also been intensified, for example, the multiple disadvantaged, and today the discrimination against roma is becoming more dangerous. the majority of the committee on employment and social affairs voted in favour of adopting the horizontal directive. we all remember the sign of parliamentary mistrust of the committee that was being formed, which became the majority opinion because of the views of one of the candidates on discrimination. the commission then promised to use the power conferred on it by section 13 of the treaty, to take legal action against any discrimination and to seek legal remedies. the commission's assessment of the transposition of existing eu legislation by member states and even the initiation of infringement procedures for failure to transpose existing directives is also urgent. the socialists cannot accept any sort of hierarchy between the prohibitions of discrimination laid down in the treaty or in the future lisbon amendment. we refuse to consider who is more harmed, the disabled, the gay, or the elderly. we believe that the legal solution proposed by the elizabeth lynne report is a general horizontal directive from the commission, which does not exclude, but rather requires, the formulation of sectoral or other specific rules. i am convinced that this is in line with the position of european citizens who are themselves victims or may be victims of discrimination. thank you very much.
my fellow officers! european unity is not only based on economics but also on a number of important values. such values include the inviolability of human dignity, freedom, responsibility, solidarity, diversity, the principle of non-discrimination, tolerance and mutual respect. it is important that, in addition to policy statements, these principles are given a legal form which guarantees the elimination of the distinction between the grounds of discrimination and provides comprehensive protection. the european project is only viable if it can ensure that the rights of disadvantaged groups are upheld. their full integration is in our common interest and must be facilitated by appropriate measures. it is therefore in the interest of all member states to ensure the full, accurate and effective transposition of all the provisions of directives 2000/43 and 2000/78 and the proper practical implementation of the directives in such a way that the transposition of the legislation into national law represents a real benefit for disadvantaged groups. laws are only valid if they are enforced. monitoring this is a highly important task which requires special attention both at member state and community level. control mechanisms for carrying out impact assessments should be developed. involve and engage in social dialogue with civil society organisations that can provide concrete feedback. i am convinced that when barroso took office in 2004, he believed what he said. please do this afterwards.

, in writing. as chair of the national intergroup on minorities, i welcome ms lynne's report, which takes stock of progress in non-discrimination and identifies the way forward. community legislation on non-discrimination is also of particular importance because protection of the individual is not sufficient. she is not discriminated against as an individual, but as a member of a group: because she is a national minority, because she is older, because she is a woman, because she is disabled, because she is a sexual minority, because she is roma. therefore, the only way to effectively protect persons belonging to different minorities is to protect the group as a whole. the report rightly points out that the two existing directives only set minimum requirements and that further progress should be made towards the development of a comprehensive anti-discrimination policy at community level, towards an anti-discrimination directive of a horizontal nature. the provision that the commission should develop a common eu-wide definition of the meaning of a positive measure is also of particular importance. the report draws particular attention to the fact that minority communities, and in particular the roma community, require special protection. in this context, i would like to recall article 25 of the zdanoka report on the same subject, which stresses the importance of the political participation of indigenous national minorities. according to the article, national minorities need a normative framework that allows them to effectively participate in the decision-making process concerning their identity through various forms of self-determination or autonomy.
in writing. −i voted in favour of mr andersson's report because i believe it is important to remove all possible obstacles to the harmonisation of qualifications. it is important to repeal council decision 85/368/eec as its implementation has not been effective in the harmonisation of professional qualifications. today, the decision is being replaced by other, newer and more effective instruments at european level, such as the european qualifications framework. the eqf covers all levels of education and training, including general and adult education, higher education and vocational education and training, as a means of promoting lifelong learning. it is therefore important that member states give greater weight to education, which is the basis for employment. education plays a major role in the development of a tolerant european society, besides providing general education and cultural skills. the next generation must learn to accept and respect otherness, in addition to energy efficiency and environmental protection. but education is more than that: it provides opportunities for minorities, roma, to preserve their culture and to integrate. even today, there are more and more well-trained youths among minorities who are prepared to defend their interests against local authorities and the government. for this to be universal, there needs to be progress in language teaching, more attention to minority cultures in schools and a more active role for minorities themselves in teaching.

there is no investigation concerning this case. on 13 may a mob of approximately 60 people used molotov cocktails to set fire to five romany camps in naples. similar violent outbursts have also occurred in other italian cities, such as milan. what is really worrying is that, according to the information i have received from the police officers in naples, there is no police investigation concerning this case either. i have to tell you that the italian government appears to be strong with those who are weak and weak with those who are strong. whenever they raise the security issue, they should first raise the organised crimes committed by the camorra. they intend to talk about the immigration and roma issue in order to divert the focus from italy’s real problems. i hope that the italian authorities will ensure adequate and effective investigation of the events which took place in naples and milan and prosecute to the fullest extent of the law all responsible persons, including public officials making hostile statements about roma which incite racial hatred. i call on the italian authorities to cooperate fully with intergovernmental institutions, international organisations and domestic civil society to swiftly and effectively end the human rights emergency of the roma in italy. i also call on the european commission to come up with an eu roma strategy with the aim of making roma inclusion an urgent priority and to provide leadership and coordination for member states in their responsibility to ensure respect for the rights of their roma citizens.

, in writing. mercury is one of the most well-known and dangerous heavy metals. it damages the nervous and immune systems, especially dangerous for babies, children. i welcome the initiative to ban exports because, despite the restrictions imposed in recent years, the use of mercury has not decreased significantly, but has shifted from the developed world to the developing world. the european union is the largest exporter in the world and it is hoped that the regulation will reduce the use of the substance worldwide. the mercury can also be used in foodstuffs which are then placed on the union market. so it is not only our global responsibility, but also our own interest to reduce mercury pollution in the third world. i consider it a success for myself and the socialist group that the ban on mercury-based potash exports is extended, since mercury can be converted into potash cheaply and the cost of recycling is minimal. without this clause, exporters of metallic mercury could easily escape compliance with the regulation.
