on behalf of the ppe-de group. - mr president, i have to go. i was in kiev recently, and on the foreign ministry building in ukraine, there was a hardly noticeable, about three stories high european flag that was flying over that building. if this symbol has earned such respect and esteem outside europe, i am convinced that it is time for the european union to take it seriously. at the same time, i would like to correct my colleague, who has produced an excellent report on this matter. the regulation of european union symbols is still part of the acquis communautaire, the body of european law. the flag with the 12 golden stars and blue stripes is part of the arc de triomphe, as much as it is the result of a meeting of heads of state and government many years ago, and as much as the european parliament is based in strasbourg, which is why we are meeting in strasbourg. so there is no regulatory vacuum at the moment, because at the moment there are regulations on the symbols of the european union, whether they are in the lisbon treaty or not. at the same time, the report also has the important aim of regulating the way in which our institution, the european parliament, respects this flag. i also believe that identity, a sense of belonging, is very important. we often talk about the fact that citizens do not understand this complicated europe. symbols are one way for them to get there and to feel closer, emotionally close to their hearts, to the european union. many people love, many people admire and many people respect these symbols, and i believe that we - here in parliament - as committed europeans have a very important task to regulate the way in which the symbols of the european union are used in this institution. thank you for your attention.
mr president, we frequently hear the opinion that symbols are a waste of time and that the european union should not concern itself with such irrelevant questions. rather, this argument goes, the european union’s job is to focus on bread-and-butter issues like employment or trade. this approach is completely mistaken and wholly misunderstands what symbols are about. if you look carefully at how institutions work, then it will rapidly become clear that every institution maintains a set of symbols. these symbols carry a message that people will recognise and act as a shorthand version of that institution. without institutions, democratic politics cannot exist. so symbols are a necessary part of democracy that the eu regards as being central to its identity. besides, if symbols were as marginal as their opponents say, then why bother to oppose them? the european parliament, as the pivotal democratic body of the european union, has every reason to promote the symbols of europe as a means of connecting itself with the voters of europe. those who are against the symbols of europe are basically questioning european democracy itself. one could further argue that this is too fluid and crisis-ridden a time to be thinking of the symbols of europe, and thus that it is not the right moment for parliament to spend time on symbols. maybe so, but timing is something that always can and will be debated. in the long run, it will not matter whether the symbols of parliament and of the european union are debated now or at some later date. so i give my full support to this important and cogent report.
in writing. allergy is a modern public health problem that has typically spread in europe since the second world war. one third of children today have allergies, and if we do nothing about it, half the population of europe will eventually suffer from this disease. chemical foods and polluted environments are responsible for allergic disease, and allergic symptoms can be triggered by chemicals, natural and artificial food additives, spices, pollen, and other natural substances, such as animal hair. in hungary, the biggest problem is the poppy. unfortunately, the european union currently has no allergy strategy, as confirmed by the commission in response to my question. the civil society organisations of allergy sufferers, the many millions of european citizens affected, rightly expect us to take action against allergy at european level. let's do something about prevention, eliminate the causes and ensure that patients are symptom-free. active action against allergy could also demonstrate that it is also concerned with the health and everyday concerns of the citizens of the union.
in writing. first of all, i am pleased to see that more and more reports and strategies have been produced recently on the importance of health. i myself, as the rapporteur for the 2009 budget, tried to make a statement on behalf of the committee on the environment and public health (envi). my initiative was well received by both the envi and the committee on budgets. as my colleague mr peter arbeloa muru points out, there is a close link between the new health threats and climate change. on 7 october the committee on budgets adopted the pilot project i had submitted entitled 'comprehensive research on health, the environment, transport and climate change - improving indoor and outdoor air quality'. this eur 4 million initiative, led by the hungarian-based regional environmental center for central and eastern europe, would be implemented in nine countries (austria, bosnia and herzegovina, finland, hungary, italy, the netherlands, norway, serbia, slovakia). the project explores the relationship and impact of health, environment, transport and climate change in schools simultaneously. i also support the rapporteur's call for the ecdc to be strengthened. to ensure that the agency can carry out its increased tasks responsibly, i have proposed to release the reserve fund set up by the european commission. this was unanimously adopted by the envi committee and will hopefully be decided by the whole of parliament at the end of october.
climate change in central europe appears to be mainly due to the radical dispersal of rainfall. long dry periods of drought are followed by torrential rains. the cost of drought and flooding could run into the billions. it also indicates that climate change has begun. european climate policies must therefore address not only the slowing down of climate change but also the prevention of the impacts that are already occurring, and mitigate the damage. we need to develop water storage, irrigation systems, and we need to conserve rainfall in cities and in the land so that we can use water in times of drought. this is particularly important in the hungarian lowlands. we need to get the european union to treat better water management as a community policy and to open up substantial eu funds in the next budget.
mr president, every year thousands of grandmothers, mothers and daughters fall victim to breast cancer. europeans in particular are affected by the disease, as the most common cancer of european women is breast cancer, with an estimated 430 000 new cases diagnosed every year. the first step to combat this terrible disease is awareness. this month, europeans must focus on early detection as the primary means of prevention. detection is a very strong, key issue in this, because in america, for example, 41% of american cases are diagnosed at a very early stage, compared to 29-30% in europe. however, 1 in 10 women in the eu will develop breast cancer before she reaches 80 years of age; in addition, every two and a half minutes another woman is diagnosed with breast cancer. the harsh reality of breast cancer is that every seven and a half minutes a woman dies of this disease. in europe, 132 000 women died of breast cancer in 2006. this october the measures to increase breast cancer awareness, early detection and prevention must come from all levels of decision-making.

opportunities for workers in the eu. it is not, of course, the case that this eu legislation establishes a harmonised framework at community level in the field of the employment of temporary agency workers. but this is neither necessary nor desirable, since, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, the basic labour law remains the responsibility of the member states in this respect. however, the desire to introduce clear and unambiguous minimum standards throughout the union, the application of which would ensure the protection of temporary agency workers throughout the community, is a welcome and positive move. i believe that, free from all ideological overtones and political affiliation, we can all agree that the protection of pregnant women, the guarantee of equal treatment for men and women, the elimination of discrimination on grounds of origin, religion, belief, membership of a minority or age, are fundamental objectives and justify the adoption of uniform rules on these issues throughout the union. this system of minimum requirements, developed by the eu legislators, is therefore intended to achieve this objective. it is important that, in addition to childcare and other infrastructure facilities. this also applies to the period between the loans. it is in the common interest of all of us that temporary agency workers should not be discriminated in writing. the hungarian public is disturbed from time to time by cases where children of hungarian parents are taken to a foreign country by a foreign spouse. public opinion is primarily sympathetic to the bereaved mother, but it is also sympathetic to the bereaved father when the child is placed in a strange and bad environment. intermarriage is increasing within the european union, but the rules on divorce and child placement are often chaotic and unclear. community law has so far only regulated the framework for disputes, such as jurisdiction, i.e. which court has jurisdiction to hear divorce or child placement proceedings. but it did not address the law applicable in marriage matters, that is, the law of the state that the court was applying in the case. the very different national laws thus created uncertainty, often forcing the parties to initiate proceedings as soon as possible so that the legislation in their favour would apply. the regulation now being drafted seeks to change this situation, in particular by favouring the agreement of the parties to the litigation. that may be appropriate for a civil divorce, but knowing the practice, i'm afraid it's not going to resolve much of the legal dispute. the correct solution would be to establish a uniform european regulation on child placement in the longer term.

thank you very much, mr. president. my fellow members of the house. i would like to draw your attention to the pressures on the maize market, which are particularly acute in central europe. the exceptionally high yields, unfavourable international market conditions and the fluctuation in yields which is considered to be traditional in central europe have resulted in prices in some hungarian regions being well below the intervention level. we need to take action to protect producers and market stability. in the current situation, hungary is not able to buy up the surplus itself, but there are other options to stabilise the market. first, the intervention buying-in of maize for non-sea-port countries should be increased. the system should in this respect fulfil the role of a safety net as originally intended. secondly, a tender for the granting of subsidies to non-sea-port countries for exports to third countries should be opened. finally, private storage facilities outside the queue should be opened. the introduction of these measures would go a long way towards preventing the situation from worsening and would help our producers to adapt to the exceptional circumstances. thank you for your attention.

in writing. it is no coincidence that the gender pay gap is a key element of the 2006-2010 roadmap for equality between women and men. the issue of the gender pay gap goes beyond the basic requirement of equal pay for equal work. the pay gap reflects the severe inequalities in the labour market, which affect women in particular. this is a serious democratic deficit in europe. the solution requires a complex action, which requires political commitment. there is a need to improve existing legislation, facilitate and monitor its practical application. the principle of genuine equality of opportunity can only be achieved if all member states show strong political will and take constructive action to close the gender pay gap. it is unacceptable that the gender pay gap in many member states still receives no differentiated attention, either in public debates or on political agendas. the launch of social dialogue and awareness-raising campaigns is also essential. i urge that a package of policy measures be developed to address this problem, taking into account national specificities and best practice in all cases. we need more detailed and more precise statistical information to identify the actual situation and to monitor the processes. the causes of wage disparities should be thoroughly investigated and the knowledge gained should be used to detect, remedy and prevent discrimination.
in writing. – i would like to congratulate ms bauer for her hard work in contributing vital recommendations to the european commission on the application of the equal pay principle. the pay gap has a major impact on the status of women in economic and social life and constitutes an obstacle to equal economic independence. there are several areas where women are affected by the gap in pay based solely on gender in europe. women encounter differences in their hourly pay in new and old member states alike. differences can be seen in the income distribution between men and women: 20 percent of women, compared to 40 percent of men in europe, receive a similar income distribution in the top-range of salaries. another glaring pay inequality lies in sectoral gender segregation, as half of jobs in three sectors are male dominated. finally, the over-representation of women – 30 percent – in part-time positions affects labour market contributions. these figures are even worse when it comes to women with certain ethnic origins, such as the roma. while the eu’s legal framework regarding equal pay is very extensive, in the european union women are still paid less then men – even those with similar skills and education – which proves that improving legislation by strengthening its effectiveness is the main goal to achieve.

on behalf of the pse group. thank you for your time, mr. president. yes, my dear fellow members of the house. as a non-econ member, allow me to approach the issue of today from a different perspective. the financial crisis in the united states last year has reached europe this year, shaking our financial markets, which until then had been considered stable, to their very foundations. instead of a single eu solution, it was a long hesitation to agree on a country-specific response that would consume hundreds of billions of euros. these individual national solutions could not be a way of dealing with the crisis for the new european member states, which are simply not able to move billions of euros on their own. they may have initially hoped that since their banks had not engaged in dubious, speculative overseas business, this international credit crisis might spare them. however, the lack of funds and the crisis of confidence associated with the international financial crisis have also greatly shaken public finances in need of foreign credit, even contributing to speculative attacks on national currencies. in this situation, it was very important that the new member states receive not only moral but also concrete, tangible financial support from eu membership, i.e. that the eu extend its protection to countries that have not yet joined the euro area. this was motivated not only by solidarity as a european principle but also by the common european interest in preventing a bank - let alone a country - from becoming insolvent, in order to avoid a domino effect. with this in mind, the aid package is now being increased from 12 billion to 25 billion. under this facility, the ecb provided a rescue package of eur 6.5 billion to hungary, which was hit hardest by the financial crisis. this was a very fair and very equitable solution, as hungary has not only been at the forefront of reforms and european integration for decades, but is also the country that, on the basis of its convergence programme adopted in 2006 and its consistent implementation, has already halved the budget deficit from around 10% last year and is reducing it to 3% this year. the eu also expects the international financial organisations to help it to continue this process of consolidation. the financial and credit crisis has now put the whole world economy in a difficult situation, which each eu member state is trying to manage individually according to its own means and very particular objectives. in order to avoid the new member states lacking such instruments from being the losers, and to ensure that europe as a whole emerges from the crisis as one, a common european economic crisis strategy would be needed in addition to the coordination of current actions. i hope that the current financial rescue package is a first step towards this, and that once it is adopted, we can concentrate all our efforts on solving the real economic crisis. thank you for the word.

in writing. immigration has long been one of the most important economic and social issues for the european union. in an ageing europe, there is a general consensus that the need to replace the workforce is essential to maintain and increase competitiveness. promoting immigration as a common response requires not only eu regulation but also a balanced strategy that takes into account both sustainable development and social balance. the report by mrs klamt on the european blue card is welcome because it provides more acceptable conditions of employment for highly qualified workers from third countries, takes account of family relations and also of the possible temporary repatriation of workers. however, i am particularly pleased that we are tackling this together with the gaubert report on the single residence and work permit, because this way we can avoid even the appearance that europe is only open to highly qualified workers. the internal social balance of the eu also requires a rethinking of the impact of a financial and economic crisis such as the current one on europe's interests. increasing unemployment itself creates social tensions, and we must therefore avoid exacerbating already existing internal ethnic and racial tensions in the context of immigration, because this not only provokes a strengthening of the far right, but in the longer term may also arouse resentment towards the eu - despite the fact that the union played only a stabilising role in the crisis.

madam president, i would like to congratulate dr gräßle. our rapporteur tried to reconcile all the parties and by doing this she could identify current problems, could find practical solutions and could find compromise. at present things are not too satisfactory. we see the court of auditors refusal to sign off the eu’s account for the 14th time in a row due to the number of irregularities and fraud cases which involve eu money. it is high time to support a tougher approach tackling the misuse of eu funds. since the establishment of the european public prosecutor’s office is postponed, it is high time to move forward in the fight against fraud by strengthening the independence of the european anti-fraud office and by strengthening the investigative power of olaf. there is an important point in the gräßle report: strengthening cooperation with the member states. although the regulation states that all national and international partners must provide all the cooperation necessary, there is no detailed legal basis for such cooperation. the number of obstacles even increases when we come to cross-border anti-fraud cooperation. there is a great need, therefore, for the amended regulation which comprises a better cooperation management between olaf and the competent authorities of the member states. the only institution which really has the means to protect the financial interest of the eu is the european parliament. if we do not stand up for the fight against corruption and fraud, there is no one to replace us. finally, i would like to raise an interesting point. while the european countries are among the so-called ‘cleanest countries’ in the 2008 global corruption perception index, according to recent studies these wealthy countries like to show preference for using illegal means, for example bribery, in their outside overseas businesses. i agree with all those who find these double standards unacceptable.
in writing. i welcome the pan-european efforts to deal with the crisis and the proposals to preserve european jobs and create new ones. i would also like to point out that countries in difficult fiscal situations, such as hungary, have no monetary or substantial fiscal or tax relief instruments to alleviate the crisis. the main ways of stimulating the economy are thus through the reallocation of european resources, their efficient use, the reduction of wage costs, the improvement of competition rules and the reduction of administrative costs. i am particularly pleased that commissioner joaquín almunia is supporting our dual efforts in this direction. i therefore attach great importance to the proposals on the structural funds, and i strongly support their adoption as soon as possible. accelerating the adoption of large projects and supporting loans to smes can contribute to stimulating demand. however, i still do not consider our efforts to help the most deprived to be sufficient, so i am proposing to include the possibility of closing down and rebuilding as part of the changes to housing subsidies. in the backward areas, in settlements, there are settlements, parts of settlements that are not suitable for renovation, and in their place, the construction of modern, energy-saving housing should be initiated. this will help to achieve the union's energy saving and job creation objectives, while also providing a significant boost to the most deprived.

the rapporteur. - my colleagues, officer. thank you for staying here to listen at this late hour. i am very pleased to present this motion for a resolution to you today. i am glad that this shows that the european parliament is interested in monitoring and improving the situation of women in the balkans. i am convinced that this is in our common interest and responsibility, because the union cannot they are not just victims but active, active, useful and indispensable elements of democratic stabilisation and reconstruction. i have never been in favour of giving women a special status, but i can say with confidence that they should be given the same opportunities as men. no more, no less than that. it's no different in this case. women can only fill this role if they are given the opportunity. and what is that option? since women make up half the population, they should be given a proportionate decision-making role. i know that many people think that quotas are not the answer, but i have to say that we have not yet found a more efficient administrative solution. the economic emancipation of women is among the first tasks. working women are more useful members of society and less vulnerable. if they do, however, they cannot be allowed to be excluded from leading positions in economic life. at the same time, if we are talking about hardworking women, we need to give them the opportunity to reconcile this with their family commitments. this requires a positive change in society's attitude towards women, and the elimination of negative stereotypes. education and the media have a prominent role to play in this. i could go on and on, but i want to highlight two very important points because of the shortness of time. first of all, we must never forget that there are several countries in a given region that cannot be grouped under one umbrella, treated in the same way. every country is working hard in every area, including improving the status of women. of course, there are those who are already ahead in this fight, and there are those who are less so. i tried to get a sense of that in the chart attached to the report. secondly, the prospect of future eu membership provides a strong motivation for these countries to achieve their objectives. it is important that this period is also fully exploited in this respect, and we shall exploit it. my report was intended to show that they are on the right track, to pay tribute to their efforts and to wish them perseverance in the future. i would like to take this opportunity to thank the secretariat of in writing. the problems of discrimination that roma communities face remain unresolved throughout europe. in both old and new member states, as well as in candidate countries, integration policies are generally weak, sporadic and administered on a case-by-case basis. romani women throughout the balkans face discrimination not only because of their gender, but also on account of the ethnic group to which they belong. due to their marginalisation from mainstream society, romani women encounter discrimination which influences their access to healthcare, quality education, housing and employment. it is extremely important that candidate and potential candidate countries in the balkans guarantee the elimination of all forms of discrimination and prejudice against romani women and introduce an effective and practical anti-discrimination strategy to be implemented at all levels (national and local). it is obvious that the european union's enlargement process, through the use of its tool the copenhagen criteria, has the potential to significantly change the situation of the roma in the balkans. for this reason it is crucial that the european commission adopt an effective monitoring system in order to measure the real efforts made in minority and women's rights in the balkans, which will then demonstrate full compliance with the political criteria for accession.
thank you, mr president. we are all committed to stopping climate change, to preventing the global environmental catastrophe that threatens humanity. of course, we also know that this has a serious cost, which we as citizens must pay sooner or later. we also know that the later we act, the greater the damage, the more irreversible the process, the more we have to pay. the question is whether we are brave enough to take the initiative and make the necessary sacrifices, and how to share the burden between the various social and economic actors. the package of legislation before us shows that the union is committed to action, but it also shows that it is unfortunately biased, inconsistent and double-dealing in its implementation. it allows some member countries to release more in 2020 than they promised in kyoto for 2010. and others, the ones who were doing well before, they don't get enough incentive. why should we ignore the fact that some member states are living up to their promises while others are making huge sacrifices? nor do i understand why the cement industry has to cut twice as much as the waste industry or transportation. it's not the brave government, minister, that dares to punish corporations harshly, it's the brave government that treats you with the same severity. not a wise government that takes its citizens' money abroad in the form of cdm, but one that invests it at home, within the eu, reducing our energy dependence by creating new, marketable, clean technologies. the current global economic recession is not a reason to stand still, but, on the contrary, an opportunity to break out. europe's historic responsibility and opportunity is to take a leading role in this third industrial revolution. thank you very much for your attention.
mr president, many of the new member states, like hungary after the end of the 1980s and the collapse of the old industry, reduced their greenhouse gas emissions. due to this process, every sixth hungarian – about a million people – lost his or her job. as the basis for the reduction here in the current proposal is 2005 and not 1990, some older member states could raise their emission values without any consequences. this is very unfair to the new member states. after the losses in our economy we need fair compensation. district heating is mainly a social question in many new member states. the decision of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety that gives free allowances for district heating in the ets is essential for us, and we must keep this achievement during the trialogue as well. i think that the use of carbon capture and storage should be judged by the market and the competition. as this is a very new and expensive technology, we have to be more cautious before subsidising it with public money. my last point is that without a proper penalty system the whole package will fail. if we do not force the member states to keep to the emission limits, all our efforts are wasted. that is why i urge this parliament to insist that we should keep our achievements concerning the penalty system because, without a penalty system, there will be no working system at all.
in writing. when we talk about developments in the eu climate package, we cannot leave out the possible consequences of the financial crisis. if governments focus their accumulated financial reserves on bank bailouts, vital energy investments may be delayed. despite the crisis, europe urgently needs to invest in the deployment of renewable energy sources and in substantial improvements in energy efficiency. we need to invest today to make renewables competitive in the near future. the commission has therefore decided to set up a european energy fund. the financial instrument in question would primarily promote energy efficiency improvements and the uptake of renewable energy sources. moreover, if we really want to strengthen the common eu energy and climate policy, this will have to be reflected in the next seven-year budget. while strengthening community support, member states should be given sufficient freedom to define their own climate policy toolbox, taking into account different regional characteristics. the temporary reduction in the price of conventional energy sources should not lull decision-makers; it should not put an end to commitments on renewable energy sources. if the european union loses sight of its own objectives in the face of the crisis, this could have a detrimental impact on its credibility and its leadership in the field of climate change.
in writing. - i agree with the rapporteur that the financial crisis does not mean that we should cut aid to the hungry in the third world. i would also point out that there are people in the european union who are also facing difficulties because of the rising food prices. and not just in the new member states, but in the old ones as well. one reason for the rise in food prices is undoubtedly the increase in biofuel production. if fuel can be sold at a higher price, it either drives out or raises the price of food that can be sold at a lower price. the eu should therefore not import biofuels from a country or a larger area where they threaten the cheap food supply of the local population. biofuels play an important role in renewable energy, but if used carelessly, they can cause serious tragedies. therefore, the european union's biofuel consumption should be based on production within the union as a matter of principle. because of the rise in local food prices and the threat to rainforests, such imports from the third world are not recommended at all.
the future of the climate package is still uncertain, as it will be decided in a few days or weeks. but i would like to emphasize one thing: district heating has been exempted from the so-called climate tax. i think this is very important. hungarian representatives, including myself, have taken the initiative to make this happen. we think that the people who live in these district heating apartments are primarily low-income people who can't afford to take on additional burdens. on the other hand, we should also be aware that district heating is an environmentally friendly heating method, since individual heating is in any case exempt from any climate tax, and i therefore think that the support thus generated should be given to modernise district heating systems. and if we use eu money to upgrade district heating in the countries of central and eastern europe, then, of course, we can no longer expect a similar discount after 2020.
on this day in december 1989, the movement began in timişoara, which led to the fall of the infamous nationalist, communist, atheist dictatorship of ceauşescu in a week with phenomenal speed. on the morning of december 15, hungarian reformed believers, standing up in defense of their church and its pastor, with surprising courage drove out the securitate and militia agents and then formed a chain of life around the church. within hours, hundreds of romanians, hungarians, germans, serbs, catholics, baptists, evangelicals, orthodox christians, and jews joined the resistance. the peaceful demonstration turned into an anti-regime and anti-communist demonstration. in 1989, timişoara in transylvania became the first free city in romania. by god's grace, faith in action has won freedom. blessed be the memory of the heroes, the martyrs and the victims. we must continue the democratic transition! from romania, the road to europe leads through thames.

we have seen in the past that there is a tremendous amount of public attention on this issue, and mostly on the elements that we are discussing, the opt-out and the time-of-care issues. there are two arguments for opt-out: one is the flexibility aspect, and the other is the free choice aspect. we pretend that the employer and the employee are in fact equal parties as if they are not equal and it is clearly one of the tasks and functions of labour law to correct this inequality. as the socialists have said, the beggar and the billionaire have the same right to sleep under the bridge. in this sense, of course, it is a free contract situation. but it's really an unequal situation, and it's not about flexibility, it's not about flexibility, it's about maintaining that inequality. the solution developed in parliament's first reading is also very helpful in terms of flexibility. you can work up to 72 hours over 26 weeks, so you can adjust to the market demands, the many orders, the many jobs. of course, you have to rest at some point, and i think that is what a directive on health and safety at work should be about. and as for business hours, if a bookstore or a clothing store doesn't have any customers or customers coming in one morning, does that mean that the shop assistant is also working in inactive hours, and that's not what counts? the correct position is that if a person does not have free time, he has to go to work, then this should be considered working time. it is another matter that the remuneration of work can be agreed by the appropriate organisations and negotiations between employers and workers. it can therefore be adapted to the realities of each country, within national jurisdiction, but working time is working time and should be treated as such. i therefore agree with the court, i do not support and i do not agree with the council's compromise.
in writing. the european union already has a rule on the division of working time. according to this, the average work week is 48 hours. in practice, this means that the worker can work 8 hours 6 days a week for an average of 4 months. i think that should be enough, because doing more work than that is detrimental to work efficiency in the long run. the council compromise, which contains rules less favourable than the existing rules and which is not supported by the governments of belgium, cyprus, spain and my country, hungary, among others, is unacceptable to the european socialists. the social europe, even in times of economic difficulties, cannot be an empty phrase. the rapporteur of the parliament, mr cercas, showed sufficient receptivity during the negotiations, allowing, for example, the 48-hour rule to be applied for an average of 12 months in order to ensure flexibility. but we cannot accept that legislation should allow for a 60- to 65-hour week or, in extreme cases, a 70- to 72-hour week. nor can the view be supported that the rules can be opt-out for an unlimited period. first of all, because the relationship between employees and employers can never be equal. i consider it hypocritical to think that the idle part of the working day is not included in working hours. i suggest that during the demonstration in front of parliament on wednesday, the day of the vote, they sit down with some of the workers and have a conversation.
the rules for using eu aid are designed to ensure that our resources are used in the best possible way and put to the best possible place. in other words, the aid must not only be spent, but investments must be made that will bring about a real change. however, to achieve these goals, we often create a bureaucratic regulatory system that tends to impede effective use, putting unnecessary burdens on both businesses and administrations. rationalizing the revenue-generating rules has a double benefit. the eu's economic stimulus aid will be more easily and in greater numbers available to smes, and the administration will be able to assess the legality of the use of the aid more quickly and easily. we must trust our entrepreneurs, our economic operators, and we can only get through this crisis by working together and helping each other. i support the proposal and i also ask the commission to continue along this path, to remove unnecessary administrative barriers from the aid system. i very much hope that this premiere will be followed by other similar good initiatives.

in writing. in december 2007, the european council invited the european commission to assess the means available for the social inclusion of roma. contrary to expectations, the published working document remained within the narrow framework of evaluation of existing community policies. the first european roma summit took place in september, without any concrete recommendations or agenda. on 8 december, the general affairs council called on the commission to report on progress achieved by early 2010. the development of a progressive community strategy can be based on the broadest possible forum, on initiatives targeting both roma and non-roma civil society, and on the combination of science, the churches and the business community. much more is needed than a presentation of good practices that are overly positive: a comprehensive strategy that addresses all areas of roma concern together, giving a clear picture of how community initiatives are implemented at local level, how they can encourage and support the most important local implementation in terms of social inclusion. a roadmap based on enforceable legal bases should be made available to member states, ensuring clear and verifiable objectives, including through sanctions. the strategy should also address issues closely related to the social inclusion of roma, such as environmental protection, health, multiple discrimination or the fight against anti-roma sentiment in the media and other areas of social life.
it was drafted by the international trade commission. − the international trade committee focused on biofuels, which have an international trade context. the international trade commission considers that international trade in biofuels, which may in this context be essentially imports from third countries, should not lead to environmental degradation and increased hunger at the global level. the international trade committee therefore recommends that member states should not take into account the use of imported biofuels that are involved in deforestation directly or indirectly imported from countries that otherwise receive international food aid or from countries that have imposed export quotas or other export restrictions on agricultural products in order to meet the biofuels target. i believe that europe is capable of producing biofuels and that the main way to reduce our energy dependence is to use biofuels produced in europe.
drafted by the committee on agriculture. - i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr turmes, on this extremely important directive. the committee on agriculture is pleased that the european commission has kept its word, because a year ago, when my report on biogas was adopted by parliament, mrs fischer-boel and i agreed that there would be no separate directive on biomass, but that the issue of biomass would be dealt with in the context of renewable energy sources. thank you for the european commission's correct observance of this. the committee on agriculture considers that biomass and biogas have a key role to play in renewable energy sources, but that biomass is unacceptable if it means deforestation and depriving crops of land suitable for food production. so bioenergy, biomass production, can never be at the expense of food production. the us bioethanol program is a negative example of the price-driving role of the us bioethanol program, which is based on corn. thank you for your attention.
i'd like to say a few words about biofuels. we know that this is a very controversial form of energy, because if we import it from the third world, it threatens to destroy the rainforest, or it induces hunger in a lot of places. at the same time, third generation biofuels in particular can also benefit our entire energy balance, so i think that we should basically produce biofuels from our own sources, basically using the surplus capacity of european agriculture for this purpose. to give you one example: in hungary, about 1 million hectares of arable land are lying fallow. if we could use these to produce biofuels in a pioneering way, while preserving the natural values of the area - and not by intensive cultivation - then we would be serving both the environment and the objective, as mr turmes said, of sourcing them primarily from our own sources, within europe...
drafted by the committee on regional development. − the proposal was also the subject of lengthy debate in parliament's committee on regional development. most of our proposals were aimed at recognising that this objective fundamentally affects the economic and social cohesion of the regions and that reducing co2 emissions can only be effectively achieved if it is integrated into cohesion policy. the greenhouse gas emission reduction considerations should be included in the conditionality of aid for structural improvements. while this commitment of the european union is binding on member state governments, it also places a heavy burden on member state local and regional authorities and other local and regional representative forums and bodies. this ambitious objective can only be achieved by continuous cooperation in the coordination and implementation of tasks, both vertically between central government and local regional bodies and horizontally between individual local regional bodies. it is a source of pleasure to see that we have moved in the right direction, but in addition to thinking in common, efforts must be stepped up in all eu member states.
on behalf of the ppe-de group. i am very sorry that the minister is not in the chamber, but my group welcomes and supports the new eu climate and energy package and, as part of it, the legislation on burden sharing between member states. this is a significant step forward for the epp-ed, as previously unregulated areas will be regulated, so that from 2013 all sources of co2 emissions will be measured and regulated. the biggest advantage of the package is that i propose that there will be a trading system in the area of 'effort sharing', which will allow us to stimulate carbon reduction through market mechanisms, as in the ets. the primary objective of the epp is to increase eco-innovation within the union, to make the eu economy the most competitive in low-carbon technologies. unfortunately, this was only partially successful, as the council and the socialist group insisted on using too much cdm - almost 80% - . this cdm amount, together with the ets, is worth 63 billion euros, which i think is exaggerated. it is also regrettable that the prime minister's decision to require european companies to reduce emissions by two and a half times as much as member states is a particularly uneven burden-sharing in the current economic climate, to the detriment of european industry. member states have also been biased against each other, failing to reward those who have delivered on commitments and failing to punish those who have undermined the union's credibility on climate. at the same time, quota trading provides significant funding opportunities for already well-performing cee countries to achieve further carbon reductions in their domestic transport and agricultural sectors, which have high savings potential. overall, this legislation is complementary and, despite its shortcomings, is a pioneering step on the european union's long journey towards a climate-sustainable, low-carbon european society.
in writing. the european union has already made a strong commitment to combating climate change by adopting the climate and energy package. the agreement reached in secret over many months is without doubt of historical significance. at the same time, we must not ignore the fact that compromise is often marked by inconsistency. while the climate package has undergone a significant change compared to the commission's proposal, the most controversial contradiction has not been resolved. the regulation adopted by the council, by incorrectly setting the base year, covers up the member states' efforts to date in the field of emissions. all of this is to say that no matter what the results are, with a new regulation, everyone can start fresh. this is not motivating decision-makers to take the necessary action, but to constantly change the rules. we find it unacceptable that the present proposal treats equally those who have reduced their emissions to a large extent and those who have even increased them. we believe that the european union is undermining its own credibility if it adopts legislation which does not take account of the fulfilment of the kyoto commitments. we believe that the eu's ambitious climate targets are a step forward. but our joy would be more complete if the agreement had not become another reminder of double standards and inconsistency.
in writing. geological storage of carbon dioxide is undoubtedly an exciting technical challenge. however, it should not be forgotten that the ultimate goal of saving the environment and mitigating climate change is to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. this means reducing the use of fossil fuels rather than finding alternatives to storing emissions underground. and this technology is very expensive. the only country where this has been achieved on a commercial scale so far is norway, and it is estimated that the cost of storage could be as high as eur 100 per tonne. that kind of money could probably be spent in more useful ways, like supporting renewable energy. it would be particularly inappropriate to use community funds to finance research in the richest member states. if geological storage of carbon dioxide is really as good a solution as its proponents claim, then it has to survive in the market, in the market competition.
on behalf of the ppe-de group. we heard about the difficulties you had to face. these difficulties, under the leadership of jutta haug, i think we have solved quite successfully. i thank him for his work and for making all the groups feel that this budget is theirs. the budget is politics in numbers. what message does the european people's party see in this budget? we believe that european citizens should be given more security, and the european union is able and ready to provide that security. about a third of its budget is devoted to objectives that enhance this sense of security. the amendments by the european people's party and the european democrats have increased this by about 1 billion euros and thus made it more visible, so to support small businesses, to preserve jobs, if possible to increase their number, to develop the backward areas, to energy security, to support the design of projects such as nabucco. but food security is also very important today, not to mention protecting the schengen borders and stopping illegal immigration. of course, as good as this budget is, it's far from perfect. some of the errors are to be found in the member states. it is unacceptable that member states do not make use of the opportunities that the union provides them, leaving billions in the union's coffers unused. some member states have created artificial barriers, making it even more difficult to access applications than we have made it ourselves. the commission's efforts to simplify these processes are welcome, but we must not allow member states to create opposing effects, opposing changes. but we must not only look to the member states for fault. the union is unable to respond quickly to the challenges of a changing world. sure, we got the food stamps, but what a fight! and now that we should be moving forward, we also have problems with the economic crisis. i think that in the next period, in the next year, we need to make substantial simplifications, both within the existing framework and in order to have more flexibility. thank you for your attention, and i hope we have a good budget.
mr. president, i have to go. a serious problem in our budget, which has been recurring for years, is the significant discrepancy between commitments and actual payments. this gap has grown to an unacceptable level this year and is already jeopardising the seriousness and meaning of the whole budgetary procedure. we simply cannot afford to do this in 2009 in the midst of the economic and financial crisis. the commission and the member states have a common duty to speed up and simplify payments and thus restore the seriousness of the european union budget. secondly, i thank my colleagues for supporting many of the important proposals in the budgetary package i have just mentioned. this package set out five main priorities: modern environmental protection (including a 10% increase in the life programme), innovative business development, the fight against corruption, progressive social policies and a substantial increase in the world's largest student exchange programme, erasmus mundus. thank you so much for supporting me on this, i think it's important stuff. finally, every year i have to point out that the funding for foreign policy objectives is insufficient. it's even more apparent in next year's budget. it was only through budgeting tricks that we were able to secure funding for the most important purposes. there is only one reassuring solution to this unfortunate situation: a real and comprehensive mid-term review. if we do not do this, it will be difficult to take seriously the european union's ambition to be a global player in the world.
as i said at its first reading in october, as rapporteur for the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, i welcome the eu budget for 2009 in several respects. on the one hand, it is significant and gratifying that next year 14 billion euros will be available for environmental protection, nature conservation and, above all, life+. on the other hand, it is worth noting that this amount is about 10% higher than last year, which shows that no one is doubting the importance of this area, especially climate change. the latter is also one of the budgetary priorities for next year. of course, all of this is tied to the southern vote today, where we're deciding on the climate package. although these draft laws fall short of our original vision in many respects, they are ambitious compared to the commission proposal published last january. and to achieve the goals they set, it takes money and political will. in total, small but important projects worth eur 7.5 million are being launched in the future. the substantive work, the tendering and the announcement are expected early next year, but the commission has already indicated on several occasions how important it is to see these projects come to fruition and to ensure maximum cooperation. since the october plenary session, only the european food safety authority (efsa) has not made substantial progress. we are still obliged to recommend that the 10% reserve should be maintained, but i hope that this problem will be solved soon. these are my observations, my suggestions, which are essentially the same as those made at first reading. i ask the house to vote in favour of these amendments now at second reading. i also congratulate jutta haug on her report. thank you very much.
in writing. – we ought to be concerned - in line with our rapporteur - about the negative effects on european citizens that will arise as a consequence of global recession. our concern should be particularly focused on the most disadvantaged of our citizen, those who will without any doubt suffer the most from the effect of the financial turmoil. the union ought to maximize its efforts in facilitating the access to available resources - within the ceilings of the agreed multiannual financial framework 2007-2013 - for beneficiaries on the ground and we should therefore particularly improve and simplify measures in order to accelerate the implementation of structural and cohesion funds. as a matter of fact, the low levels of payments in the implementation of cohesion policy do not reflect the needs on the ground when facing the challenges of the current economic crisis. cohesion policy is the greatest instrument of solidarity in the union and its role in tackling the negative effects of a global crisis of this scale is essential.
i voted for the compromise version of the doyle report because important amendments were incorporated into the legislation. i'm the one who introduced the amendment that would give district heating a free quota, exempting community heating from the climate tax. this is an important result, as district heating is used primarily by people with lower incomes, and is also more environmentally friendly than individual heating. i am also pleased that the heat and cooling produced by cogeneration plants are also given free quota. that's another green thinking. many activities are exempted from paying the climate tax that should not be exempted. i would have preferred a greener law on my part. but this compromise is better than nothing.
i voted in favour of the climate package, although it's hard to see a clear positive. there is no doubt that this legislation is progressive, that it fills a gap and that it makes the world a rarity. but the targets that have been set do not reflect what our scientists have set as goals to stop climate change, humanity's greatest challenge. europe's task is to build a model of a low-carbon economy and society, as europe has one of the greatest potential to create the technology needed to do so. but that requires money and legal coercion. however, this decision takes a significant part of the money out of the union in the form of cdms, and our legislation has too many exceptions, too many flexibilities and too little enforceability. in sum, we are moving in the right direction, aware of our responsibilities and the magnitude of our tasks, but not at the right pace, and so my vote was for the right direction, not the speed of progress.
the main problem with this legislation is that some central and eastern european countries have already significantly reduced their greenhouse gas emissions since the late 1980s. some western countries, however, have increased the same to a great extent even in the early 2000's. that's why we asked for a fair deal, a recognition of our past efforts. we got something, but you have to know that the eu's climate assessment would be much worse without the efforts of the new member states. it would therefore be important for the old member states to benefit from the quota trade. in particular, they should transfer part of their industry or buy quotas from less developed countries within the union. the compromise was agreed for climate protection and common european action. i also understand the concerns of environmentalists and i hope that we can tighten up the legislation later.
i am very disappointed that we were unable to accept the council's position at second reading. we need to be competitive. there is no slave labor here, even if someone does, they can only work 60-65 hours a week at most. instead, we have taken a course of total inflexibility, with immediate compensation time, which makes it completely impossible for employers to have a position on seasonal work, for example. and i just want to point out to my colleagues that the very well protected worker who is unemployed is far better off than the person who is hired by the employers and entrusted with the conditions on which he accepts employment. so i'm very desperate about the opt-out. as for the time limit, i voted for amendment no 9 because it seemed that we were going to reconciliation, and because it could be regulated at national level by the amendment.
i welcome the commission's latest human rights report. the main reason is that the protection of minorities has also given attention to the roma, the largest transnational minority in europe. however, i find it interesting that, although there is an increase in racist and ethnic crimes and offenses against roma in western and central europe, the report focuses more on the balkans and south-eastern europe. not only in the countries that want to join, but also in our own home, there is work to be done! i also believe that there is still a lack of a transnational initiative to tackle the problems of transnational minorities together. the rights of a community of 10 million people, in my opinion, should have been included in the report, emphasising that it is not a homogeneous ethnicity. i also miss a study on the social effects of the economic crisis, because it is always the vulnerable who are at risk! however, i find the development of the disability issue in europe to be remarkable. the commission is already working on draft council decisions on the adoption of the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities and its optional protocol of 8 august 2008. the convention represents a major change in content, as it treats disability not as a social, welfare, but as a human rights and legal issue. this will help 650 million people with disabilities worldwide, including 50 million europeans. i consider it important to promote,protect and guarantee the full and equal enjoyment of all human rights and fundamental freedoms by our fellow citizens with disabilities and to promote respect for their inalienable dignity.
on behalf of the ppe-de group. it is a difficult task for the european parliament to take a comprehensive decision on the situation of fundamental rights in the union, since it is precisely fundamental rights that should be the least politically and partisan-driven, and much more based on independent facts and data, stripping away hypocrisy. it is therefore a serious message from parliament, from us, that we have created the fundamental rights agency in the period under review, which may have to do just that, and that will be its task in the years to come. at the same time, it is of course very important to know what message parliament is sending to the citizens of europe about their fundamental rights. is it addressing their problems, and is it really addressing the real problems that affect so many people? the diversity of the individual problematic and controversial issues is also indicated by the 240 amendments to the draft report. the original, highly controversial report has been forged into a more acceptable text, thanks to the rapporteur and his shadow rapporteurs. this does not mean that there would not still be a difference in principle between the political groups, as there are large differences in paragraphs reflecting sensitive, principled and emotional attitudes many of them in member states' competences. in the western half of europe, the concept of fundamental rights is almost always understood as the absence of discrimination, whether on the grounds of a large migrant minority, ethnicity or sexual orientation. however, in the new member states, we are still in a different place when it comes to enforcing our fundamental rights. we are still afraid of our fundamental freedoms in many cases, and when we talk about minorities, we are talking about millions of indigenous minorities whose fundamental rights are still being violated in the new member states. it's important that this report finally addresses these issues. however, the people's party does not consider acceptable a number of formulations which are still debated today and do not have a unified assessment. but remember: the protection of our fundamental rights may vary from person to person. the most important thing in their treatment is respect for each other - that's what's gonna help in this case.
